| 414 | 4. Birth of Scientific Psychology | 1898 | W.H.R. Rivers: Cambridge Torres Straits Field Expedition | W.H.R. Rivers, Myers & McDougall | First cross-cultural experimental psychology fieldwork testing visual illusions in Melanesia. | `cultural_psych` |
| 415 | 4. Birth of Scientific Psychology | 1899 | Carl Seashore: Measures of Musical Talent Auditory Battery | Carl Seashore, Univ of Iowa | Pioneers psychometric battery measuring pitch discrimination, rhythm, timbre, and tonal memory. | `experimental_psych` |
| 416 | 4. Birth of Scientific Psychology | 1899 | Thorstein Veblen: Theory of the Leisure Class | Thorstein Veblen, Chicago | Analyzes psychological motivation of conspicuous consumption and social status signaling. | `social_psych` |
| 417 | 4. Birth of Scientific Psychology | 1899 | Willard Small: Introduction of the White Rat Maze | Willard Stanton Small, Clark University | Builds a scale model of the Hampton Court hedge maze to study spatial learning in white rats. | `behaviorism` |
| 418 | 4. Birth of Scientific Psychology | 1900 | Ernst Mach: Knowledge and Error in Psychology of Discovery | Ernst Mach, Vienna | Analyzes role of sensory trial-and-error, mental models, and thought experiments in scientific reasoning. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 419 | 4. Birth of Scientific Psychology | 1900 | Karl Pearson: The Chi-Square Goodness-of-Fit Test | Karl Pearson, University College London | Introduces chi-square distribution for testing psychological and biological frequency distributions. | `experimental_psych` |
| 420 | 4. Birth of Scientific Psychology | 1900 | Wilhelm Wundt: Völkerpsychologie Volume 1 Published | Wilhelm Wundt, Univ of Leipzig | Launches ten-volume cultural psychology series examining language, myth, and custom beyond lab limits. | `cultural_psych` |

## 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution (#421 – #565)

| # | Era | Year / Period | Topic Title | Key Figure(s) / Institution | 1-Sentence Core Summary | Subfield |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 421 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1895 | Freud and Breuer: Studies on Hysteria Published | Sigmund Freud & Josef Breuer, Vienna | Foundational text of psychoanalysis: introducing psychic trauma, resistance, and the cathartic method. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 422 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1895 | Freuds Project for a Scientific Psychology | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Ambitious attempt to map mental processes, memory, and repression directly onto neurobiological circuits. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 423 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1896 | Freud Coins the Term Psychoanalysis | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | First uses psychoanalysis in French paper, abandoning hypnotic suggestion for free association. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 424 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1896 | Sigmund Freud: The Aetiology of Hysteria Lecture | Sigmund Freud, Vienna Psychiatric Society | Presents the early trauma seduction hypothesis of hysteria, met with dismissal by Krafft-Ebing. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 425 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1897 | Freuds Letter to Fliess: Seduction Theory Abandoned | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Historic turning point: realizes patients accounts of childhood seduction are unconscious wish fantasies. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 426 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1897 | Freuds Self-Analysis and the Oedipus Complex | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Introspecting his own grief over father death reveals universal subconscious myth of Oedipus Rex. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 427 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1898 | Sigmund Freud: Forgetting the Name of Painter Signorelli | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | First published case of motivated forgetting driven by repressed associations with death and sexuality. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 428 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1899 | Freud: Screen Memories (Deckerinnerungen) | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Demonstrates early childhood memories are often harmless retroactive psychological shields masking trauma. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 429 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1900 | Freud: The Interpretation of Dreams Masterpiece | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Declares dreams the royal road to the unconscious; decodes latent dream thoughts via manifest imagery. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 430 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1900 | Hugo Liepmann: Clinical Discovery of Motor Apraxia | Hugo Karl Liepmann, Berlin | Identifies patients with intact strength who cannot execute learned purposeful movements on command. | `neuropsychology` |
| 431 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1901 | Freud: The Psychopathology of Everyday Life | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Analyzes parapraxes (Freudian slips), forgotten names, and bungled actions as unconscious motives leaking out. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 432 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1901 | Külpe & Marbe: Würzburg School Imageless Thought | Oswald Külpe & Karl Marbe, Univ of Würzburg | Systematic introspection proves judgment and thought occur without sensory images (imageless thought). | `cognitive_psych` |
| 433 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1901 | Wilhelm Stekel: The Psychology of Kleptomania | Wilhelm Stekel, Vienna | First psychoanalytic study of compulsive stealing as symbolic substitute for forbidden sexual gratification. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 434 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1902 | Charles Horton Cooley: The Looking-Glass Self Theory | Charles Horton Cooley, Univ of Michigan | Proposes self-identity forms by imagining how our appearance and actions are judged by others. | `social_psych` |
| 435 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1902 | Paul Dubois: Rational Psychotherapy of Nervous Disorders | Paul Dubois, Bern Switzerland | Pioneers rational persuasion and cognitive retraining to treat functional neuroses without hypnosis. | `clinical_psych` |
| 436 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1902 | Wednesday Psychological Society Founded in Vienna | Freud, Adler, Stekel & Kahane | Weekly discussions in Freuds apartment evolve into the Vienna Psychoanalytic Society. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 437 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1902 | William James: The Varieties of Religious Experience | William James, Gifford Lectures Edinburgh | Landmark psychological study of mystical states, conversion crises, and the sick vs healthy soul. | `philosophy_of_mind` |
| 438 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1903 | Ivan Pavlov: Conditioned Reflexes Announced at Madrid | Ivan Pavlov, International Congress | Demonstrates psychic secretion: dogs salivate to metronome previously paired with meat powder. | `behaviorism` |
| 439 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1903 | Otto Weininger: Sex and Character Psychology Controversy | Otto Weininger, Vienna | Notorious controversial treatise on psychological bisexuality, male-female character types, and tragedy. | `philosophy_of_mind` |
| 440 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1903 | Pierre Janet: Obsessions and Psychasthenia Monograph | Pierre Janet, Collège de France Paris | Attributes obsessive doubts, phobias, and tics to a drop in integrative psychological tension. | `clinical_psych` |
| 441 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1903 | Walter Dill Scott: The Theory of Advertising Psychology | Walter Dill Scott, Northwestern Univ | Pioneers consumer psychology by applying mental suggestion and attention laws to print advertising. | `industrial_org` |
| 442 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1904 | Carl Jung: Word Association Studies at Burgholzli | Carl Gustav Jung, Zurich | Measures galvanic skin response and hesitation to words, proving existence of feeling-toned complexes. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 443 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1904 | Charles Spearman: General Intelligence (g-Factor) Paper | Charles Spearman, London | Invents factor analysis and discovers positive manifold correlations pointing to general intelligence (g). | `experimental_psych` |
| 444 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1904 | G. Stanley Hall: Adolescence and Storm and Stress | G. Stanley Hall, Clark University | Two-volume classic defining adolescence as a tumultuous evolutionary rebirth marked by Sturm und Drang. | `developmental_psych` |
| 445 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1905 | Alfred Binet and Theodore Simon: First Practical IQ Scale | Alfred Binet & Theodore Simon, Paris | Develops mental age test to identify French schoolchildren requiring specialized educational assistance. | `experimental_psych` |
| 446 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1905 | Freud: Fragment of an Analysis of a Case of Hysteria (Dora) | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Classic case history analyzing unconscious transference, somatic conversion, and adolescent resistance. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 447 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1905 | Freud: Jokes and Their Relation to the Unconscious | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Shows humor acts as an economy of psychic expenditure, bypassing social repression of taboo aggression. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 448 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1905 | Freud: Psychopathic Characters on the Stage | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Analyzes theatrical tragedy as safe psychological space where spectators project repressed neurotic drives. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 449 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1905 | Freud: Three Essays on the Theory of Sexuality | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Outlines infantile sexuality and psychosexual developmental stages: oral, anal, phallic, latency, genital. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 450 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1905 | Mary Whiton Calkins Elected First Female APA President | Mary Whiton Calkins, Wellesley | Elected 14th APA president, advocating self-psychology studying the unified conscious person. | `philosophy_of_mind` |
| 451 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1905 | Narziß Ach: Determining Tendencies and Mental Set | Narziß Ach, Würzburg School Germany | Demonstrates unconscious task goals (determining tendencies) automatically guide conscious responses. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 452 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1906 | Alois Alzheimer: Clinical Case of Auguste Deter | Alois Alzheimer, Tubingen Meeting | Links 51-year-old Auguste Deter's severe dementia to neurofibrillary tangles and cortical plaques. | `neuropsychology` |
| 453 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1906 | Charles Sherrington: Integrative Action of Nervous System | Sir Charles Sherrington, Yale Lectures | Explains reciprocal innervation, proprioception, and how excitatory and inhibitory synapses integrate. | `neuropsychology` |
| 454 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1906 | Eugen Bleuler: Coining Schizophrenia to Replace Dementia | Eugen Bleuler, Burgholzli Zurich | Reframes disorder around four As: loosened associations, blunted affect, autism, and ambivalence. | `clinical_psych` |
| 455 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1906 | First Correspondence Between Freud and Carl Jung | Sigmund Freud & Carl Jung | Begins intense seven-year intellectual partnership between the Viennese master and Zurich psychiatrist. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 456 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1907 | Alfred Adler: Study of Organ Inferiority and Compensation | Alfred Adler, Vienna | Argues bodily weaknesses provoke psychological overcompensation, anticipating inferiority complex. | `clinical_psych` |
| 457 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1907 | Carl Jung: The Psychology of Dementia Praecox | Carl Gustav Jung, Burgholzli | Applies psychoanalytic drive theory to schizophrenia, showing bizarre delusions possess symbolic logic. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 458 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1907 | Maria Montessori: First Casa dei Bambini Opened in Rome | Maria Montessori, San Lorenzo Rome | Pioneers self-directed sensory education and identifies sensitive periods in early child development. | `developmental_psych` |
| 459 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1907 | Oskar Pfungst: Clever Hans and Experimenter Cue Bias | Oskar Pfungst & Carl Stumpf, Berlin | Proves the arithmetic horse Clever Hans responds to subtle involuntary posture cues from questioners. | `experimental_psych` |
| 460 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1907 | Sigmund Freud: Delusions and Dreams in Jensen's Gradiva | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | First psychoanalytic analysis of a novel, decoding archaeologist Hanold's Pompeii delusion and dreams. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 461 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | Clifford Beers: A Mind That Found Itself Reform Memoir | Clifford Whittington Beers, New Haven CT | Former asylum patient exposes brutal institutional abuse, launching the Mental Hygiene Movement. | `clinical_psych` |
| 462 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | First International Psychoanalytic Congress at Salzburg | 42 Analysts from 6 Countries | First formal global meeting of psychoanalysts, establishing international journal of psychoanalysis. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 463 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | Hugo Munsterberg: On the Witness Stand Forensic Psychology | Hugo Munsterberg, Harvard | Exposes unreliability of eyewitness testimony, false confessions, and the psychological power of suggestion. | `social_psych` |
| 464 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | Karl Abraham: Psychosexual Differences in Melancholia | Karl Abraham, Berlin | Links manic-depressive illness to fixation at oral-sadistic stage of infantile psychosexual development. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 465 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | Margaret Floy Washburn: The Animal Mind Textbook | Margaret Floy Washburn, Vassar College | Synthesizes experimental studies on sensory discrimination, spatial memory, and learning across animals. | `evolutionary_psych` |
| 466 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | Sandor Ferenczi: Introjection and Transference Published | Sandor Ferenczi, Budapest | Formulates introjection: neurotic individuals absorb external objects into the ego to manage trauma. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 467 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | William McDougall: Introduction to Social Psychology | William McDougall, London | Proposes instinct theory: human social behaviors are organized around innate evolutionary instincts. | `social_psych` |
| 468 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1908 | Yerkes & Dodson: Inverted-U Arousal Performance Law | Robert M. Yerkes & John D. Dodson, Harvard | Demonstrates learning peaks at moderate arousal levels and degrades under extreme anxiety on hard tasks. | `experimental_psych` |
| 469 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1909 | Edward B. Titchener: Coining the English Word Empathy | Edward Bradford Titchener, Cornell Univ | Translates Theodor Lipps's German concept Einfühlung into English as the psychological term Empathy. | `social_psych` |
| 470 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1909 | Ernest Jones: On the Nightmare and Unconscious Demonology | Ernest Jones, London & Toronto | Psychoanalytic exploration of sleep paralysis, incubus myths, and repressed sexual terror in dreams. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 471 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1909 | Freud and Jung: Clark University Lectures in America | Freud, Jung & G. Stanley Hall | Freud delivers five lectures introducing psychoanalysis to the American scientific medical community. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 472 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1909 | Freud: Analysis of Phobia in Five-Year-Old Boy (Little Hans) | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Interprets boys fear of biting horses as displaced castration anxiety rooted in Oedipal rivalry. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 473 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1909 | Freud: Notes Upon a Case of Obsessional Neurosis (Rat Man) | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Classic clinical analysis of severe obsessive rituals, compulsive guilt, and unconscious ambivalence. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 474 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1909 | Korbinian Brodmann: 52 Cytoarchitectonic Cortical Areas | Korbinian Brodmann, Berlin | Maps the cerebral cortex into 52 numbered Brodmann areas based on six-layer cellular organization. | `neuropsychology` |
| 475 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1909 | Otto Rank: The Myth of the Birth of the Hero | Otto Rank, Vienna | Applies psychoanalytic Oedipal archetypes to comparative mythology across Greek, Hebrew, and Indian lore. | `cultural_psych` |
| 476 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1910 | International Psychoanalytical Association (IPA) Founded | Carl Jung Elected First President | Freud appoints Jung as crown prince and first president of the global IPA in Nuremberg. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 477 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1910 | Lou Andreas-Salome: Female Narcissism and Psychoanalysis | Lou Andreas-Salome, Vienna | Brilliant intellectual joins Freuds circle, analyzing female narcissism as healthy self-preservation. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 478 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1910 | Sigmund Freud: Leonardo da Vinci Psychobiography | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Pioneers psychobiography by analyzing Leonardo's childhood kite memory and artistic sublimation. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 479 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1911 | Alfred Adler Breaks with Freud: Individual Psychology | Alfred Adler, Vienna | Rejects Freuds pan-sexualism, founding Individual Psychology centered on inferiority and social interest. | `clinical_psych` |
| 480 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1911 | Freud: Formulations on the Two Principles of Mental | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Formulates the Pleasure Principle (immediate gratification) vs Reality Principle (delayed adjustment). | `psychoanalysis` |
| 481 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1911 | Sabina Spielrein: Destruction as Cause of Coming Into Being | Sabina Spielrein, Vienna Psychoanalytic | Historic paper introducing the destructive impulse in human sexuality, directly inspiring Freuds death drive. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 482 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1911 | Sigmund Freud: Analysis of the Paranoid Schreber Case | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Analyzes Judge Daniel Paul Schreber's memoirs, interpreting paranoid delusions as defensive projection. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 483 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1911 | Édouard Claparède: Pin-in-Hand Implicit Memory Test | Édouard Claparède, Univ of Geneva | Amnesic Korsakoff patient pricked by a hidden pin forgets the event but refuses to shake hands again. | `neuropsychology` |
| 484 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1912 | Alfred Adler: The Neurotic Constitution Published | Alfred Adler, Vienna | Argues neurosis is driven by exaggerated masculine protest and compensatory striving for godlike superiority. | `clinical_psych` |
| 485 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1912 | Carl Jung: Psychology of Unconscious and Break with Freud | Carl Gustav Jung, Zurich | Broadens libido into general psychic energy, triggering painful irreversible rupture with Freud. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 486 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1912 | Freud: Evenly Suspended Attention in Clinical Technique | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Advises analysts to maintain neutral, evenly suspended attention as a reflective mirror to the patient. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 487 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1912 | Max Wertheimer: Experimental Studies on the Seeing of Motion | Max Wertheimer, Frankfurt | Discovers phi phenomenon: flashing stationary lights evoke apparent motion, igniting Gestalt psychology. | `experimental_psych` |
| 488 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1912 | William Stern: Coining the Intelligence Quotient (IQ) | William Stern, Breslau | Formulates IQ as mental age divided by chronological age multiplied by 100 (IQ = MA / CA * 100). | `developmental_psych` |
| 489 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1913 | Carl Jung: Introducing Archetypes of the Collective | Carl Gustav Jung, London | Distinguishes personal repressed unconscious from universal phylogenetic archetypes shared across humanity. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 490 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1913 | Freud: Totem and Taboo and Cultural Oedipal Myth | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Hypothesizes prehistoric murder of tyrannical primal father as root of guilt, incest taboos, and religion. | `cultural_psych` |
| 491 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1913 | Hugo Munsterberg: Psychology and Industrial Efficiency | Hugo Munsterberg, Harvard | Founds industrial/organizational psychology: worker selection, job monotony, and advertising psychology. | `industrial_org` |
| 492 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1913 | John B. Watson: Psychology as the Behaviorist Views It | John B. Watson, Columbia Univ | The Behaviorist Manifesto: rejects introspection and consciousness, demanding study of observable behavior. | `behaviorism` |
| 493 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1913 | Karl Jaspers: General Psychopathology Phenomenology | Karl Jaspers, Heidelberg Clinic | Founds phenomenological psychiatry, separating empathetic understanding from causal neurobiology. | `clinical_psych` |
| 494 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1913 | Maximilien Ringelmann: Social Loafing Rope-Pulling Study | Maximilien Ringelmann, Paris | Discovers individuals exert progressively less physical pulling force as group size increases. | `social_psych` |
| 495 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1914 | Carl Jung: Resignation from IPA and The Red Book Inward | Carl Gustav Jung, Kusnacht | Enters profound confrontation with the unconscious, chronicling visionary archetypal figures in Liber Novus. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 496 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1914 | Freud: On Narcissism: An Introduction | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Distinguishes primary narcissism from secondary libido withdrawal into ego in melancholia and paranoia. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 497 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1914 | Freud: Remembering, Repeating and Working-Through | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Shows patients who cannot consciously recall repressed trauma act it out behaviorally in transference. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 498 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1914 | Leta Stetter Hollingworth: Debunking Menstrual Deficit | Leta Stetter Hollingworth, Columbia Univ | Empirical motor and cognitive tests refute the myth that menstruation impairs women's mental ability. | `experimental_psych` |
| 499 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1914 | Sandor Ferenczi: Development of the Sense of Reality | Sandor Ferenczi, Budapest | Traces infant progression from magical omnipotence to mature cognitive adaptation to external reality. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 500 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1915 | Charles Samuel Myers: Coining Shell Shock in The Lancet | Charles Samuel Myers, British Army | Publishes the first medical reports of combat blindness, mutism, and amnesia under trench shelling. | `clinical_psych` |
| 501 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1915 | Edgar Rubin: The Rubin Vase Figure-Ground Reversal | Edgar Rubin, Univ of Copenhagen | Demonstrates visual perception bistably alternates between a central vase and two facing silhouettes. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 502 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1915 | Freud: The Unconscious and Instincts and Their Vicissitudes | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Metapsychological essays defining instinct aims, repression dynamics, and primary unconscious processes. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 503 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1915 | Freud: Thoughts for the Times on War and Death | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Reflects on WW1 devastation, showing civilization veneer peels off instantly to unleash primitive savagery. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 504 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1916 | Carl Jung: Active Imagination and Transcendent Function | Carl Gustav Jung, Zurich | Develops Active Imagination method for conscious ego dialogue with spontaneous unconscious symbols. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 505 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1916 | Freud: Introductory Lectures on Psycho-Analysis in Vienna | Sigmund Freud, Univ of Vienna | Comprehensive lecture series introducing parapraxes, dream symbolism, and libido theory to general audience. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 506 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1916 | Lewis Terman: The Stanford-Binet Intelligence Scale | Lewis Terman, Stanford Univ | Standardizes Binet scale on American norms, popularizing IQ scoring in schools and clinical settings. | `developmental_psych` |
| 507 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1917 | Freud: Mourning and Melancholia Published | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Contrasts conscious grief with clinical melancholia where unconscious fury against lost object turns inward. | `clinical_psych` |
| 508 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1917 | Georg Groddeck: The Book of the It and Somatic Psychosis | Georg Groddeck, Baden-Baden | Pioneers psychosomatic psychoanalysis: the unconscious It (Das Es) expresses inner psychic conflict via organic disease. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 509 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1917 | Lillian & Frank Gilbreth: Applied Motion Study Ergonomics | Lillian Moller Gilbreth, Providence RI | Pioneers human factors engineering, Therblig motion units, and fatigue reduction in industrial work. | `industrial_org` |
| 510 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1917 | Robert Yerkes: Army Alpha and Beta Tests for WW1 Recruits | Robert Yerkes & APA Committee | First mass psychological intelligence testing of 1.7 million soldiers, sparking testing boom and controversy. | `industrial_org` |
| 511 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1917 | W.H.R. Rivers: Treating Shell Shock at Craiglockhart | W.H.R. Rivers & Siegfried Sassoon | Applies talking cure and dream analysis to traumatized WW1 soldiers, validating psychological war neurosis. | `clinical_psych` |
| 512 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1918 | Freud: From the History of an Infantile Neurosis (Wolf Man) | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Examines Sergei Pankejeffs childhood nightmare of white wolves, analyzing primal scene observation. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 513 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1918 | Karl Abraham: The Oral Phase of Melancholia Development | Karl Abraham, Berlin | Deepens depression theory: links manic-depressive mood swings to primal oral envy and introjected love objects. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 514 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1918 | Robert S. Woodworth: Personal Data Sheet Neurotic Screen | Robert Sessions Woodworth, Columbia Univ | Creates the world's first standardized self-report personality inventory to screen WW1 recruits. | `clinical_psych` |
| 515 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1919 | Freud: The Uncanny (Das Unheimliche) Aesthetic Psychology | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Analyzes psychological creepiness of automata, doubles, and severed limbs as repressed familiar things returning. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 516 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1919 | Victor Tausk: The Influencing Machine in Schizophrenia | Victor Tausk, Vienna Psychoanalytic Society | Analyzes psychotic delusions of being controlled by remote machines as projected bodily estrangement. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 517 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1920 | Berlin Psychoanalytic Polyclinic Free Therapy Founded | Karl Abraham & Max Eitingon, Berlin | Opens the first free outpatient psychoanalytic clinic and establishes the tripartite training model. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 518 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1920 | Edward Thorndike: Discovery of the Halo Rating Bias | Edward L. Thorndike, Columbia Univ | Coins the Halo Effect after showing raters let one global impression skew all specific trait scores. | `social_psych` |
| 519 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1920 | Francis Cecil Sumner: First Black PhD in Psychology | Francis Cecil Sumner, Clark University | Earns doctorate under G. Stanley Hall and builds Howard University's landmark psychology department. | `cultural_psych` |
| 520 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1920 | Freud: Beyond the Pleasure Principle and the Death Drive | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Introduces Thanatos (death drive) to explain war neurosis flashbacks and self-destructive repetition compulsion. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 521 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1920 | John B. Watson and Rosalie Rayner: Little Albert Experiment | John B. Watson & Rosalie Rayner | Conditions 9-month-old Little Albert to fear white rat and rabbit via loud steel bar clangs, proving fear conditioning. | `behaviorism` |
| 522 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1920 | Wilhelm Reich: Early Formulations on the Genital Character | Wilhelm Reich, Vienna Psychoanalytic | Argues orgastic potency and release of somatic sexual energy are essential biological criteria for mental health. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 523 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1921 | A.S. Neill: Founds Summerhill Progressive School | A.S. Neill, Suffolk England | Creates democratic anti-authoritarian school guided by psychoanalytic principles of self-regulation and free play. | `developmental_psych` |
| 524 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1921 | Carl Jung: Psychological Types Published | Carl Gustav Jung, Zurich | Classifies personality into introversion and extraversion crossed with thinking, feeling, sensation, and intuition. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 525 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1921 | Ernst Kretschmer: Physique and Character Somatotypes | Ernst Kretschmer, Univ of Tubingen | Correlates asthenic body builds with schizoid traits and pyknic builds with manic-depressive moods. | `clinical_psych` |
| 526 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1921 | Freud: Group Psychology and the Analysis of the Ego | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Explores crowd psychology: how followers surrender their ego ideal to charismatic leaders in churches and armies. | `social_psych` |
| 527 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1921 | Hermann Rorschach: Psychodiagnostik and the Inkblot Test | Hermann Rorschach, Herisau | Designs 10 symmetrical inkblot cards to diagnose schizophrenia and personality via unconscious projection. | `clinical_psych` |
| 528 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1921 | Otto Loewi: Vagusstoff Chemical Synaptic Transmission | Otto Loewi, Univ of Graz Austria | Dream-inspired frog heart experiment proves nerves communicate across synapses via acetylcholine. | `neuropsychology` |
| 529 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1922 | Freud and Paul Federn: Discussions on Ego Boundaries | Paul Federn & Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Federn introduces the concept of fluctuating ego boundaries (Ich-Grenzen) to explain depersonalization. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 530 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1922 | Karen Horney: Genesis of the Castration Complex in Women | Karen Horney, Berlin Psychoanalytic | First psychoanalytic critique of Freudian penis envy, arguing male dread of women drives female subjugation. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 531 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1922 | Walter Lippmann: Public Opinion and Cognitive Stereotypes | Walter Lippmann, New York | Introduces the term Stereotype as simplified mental pictures in our heads that filter social reality. | `social_psych` |
| 532 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1923 | Freud: The Ego and the Id Structural Model of Psyche | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Structural tripartite agency replaces topography: primitive Id, rational Ego, and moral Superego. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 533 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1923 | Jean Piaget: The Language and Thought of the Child | Jean Piaget, Rousseau Institute Geneva | Discovers egocentric speech in young children, initiating genetic epistemology of cognitive stages. | `developmental_psych` |
| 534 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1923 | Joseph Capgras: The Impostor Delusion (Capgras Syndrome) | Joseph Capgras & Jean Reboul-Lachaux, Paris | Describes patients who recognize loved ones' faces but insist they have been replaced by impostors. | `neuropsychology` |
| 535 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1923 | Max Wertheimer: Laws of Perceptual Grouping Published | Max Wertheimer, Berlin | Formulates Gestalt grouping principles of proximity, similarity, continuity, closure, and Prägnanz. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 536 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1923 | Sandor Ferenczi: Thalassa: A Theory of Genitality | Sandor Ferenczi, Budapest | Speculative psychoanalytic bio-analysis tracing erotic drives back to evolutionary catastrophe of oceanic drying. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 537 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1924 | Floyd Allport: Social Psychology Textbook Published | Floyd Allport, Syracuse University | Rejects group mind mysticism, grounding social psychology in experimental study of interacting individuals. | `social_psych` |
| 538 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1924 | Hans Berger: First Human Electroencephalogram (EEG) | Hans Berger, Univ of Jena Germany | Records electrical alpha and beta brainwaves from the human scalp, inventing electroencephalography. | `neuropsychology` |
| 539 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1924 | Jenkins & Dallenbach: Sleep Protects Memory Retention | John Jenkins & Karl Dallenbach, Cornell | Proves nonsense syllables learned prior to sleep are recalled far better than after waking intervals. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 540 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1924 | Mary Cover Jones: Peter and Counterconditioning of Phobia | Mary Cover Jones, Columbia Univ | Mother of behavior therapy: extinguishes 3-year-old Peters rabbit fear by pairing animal with favorite snacks. | `behaviorism` |
| 541 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1924 | Otto Rank: The Trauma of Birth and Rupture with Freud | Otto Rank, Vienna | Argues primary psychological trauma is biological expulsion from womb, creating severe split with Freud. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 542 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1925 | Anna Freud: Lectures on the Technique of Child Analysis | Anna Freud, Vienna | Develops specialized child psychoanalytic technique using alliance building rather than direct adult transference. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 543 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1925 | Lev Vygotsky: The Psychology of Art and Catharsis | Lev Vygotsky, Moscow | Analyzes how aesthetic structure transforms conflicting emotions into higher cognitive resolution. | `cultural_psych` |
| 544 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1925 | Wolfgang Kohler: The Mentality of Apes and Insight Learning | Wolfgang Kohler, Tenerife Station | Chimpanzee Sultan solves multi-stick fruit retrieval tasks through sudden cognitive insight (Aha-Erlebnis). | `cognitive_psych` |
| 545 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1926 | Florence Goodenough: Draw-A-Man Child Intelligence Test | Florence Goodenough, Univ of Minnesota | Develops a standardized nonverbal assessment scoring children's human figure drawings for maturity. | `developmental_psych` |
| 546 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1926 | Freud: Inhibitions, Symptoms and Anxiety | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Revises anxiety doctrine: ego produces signal anxiety to mobilize defense mechanisms against internal danger. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 547 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1926 | Freud: The Question of Lay Analysis | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Defends psychoanalysis as an independent human science that should not be monopolized by medical doctors. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 548 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1926 | Graham Wallas: Four Stages of Creative Thought | Graham Wallas, London School of Economics | Codifies creative problem solving into Preparation, Incubation, Illumination, and Verification. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 549 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1926 | Melanie Klein: The Psycho-Analysis of Children and Play | Melanie Klein, London | Pioneers play therapy, analyzing young children toy choices as direct expressions of unconscious phantasy. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 550 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1926 | Paul Schilder: The Image and Appearance of the Human Body | Paul Schilder, Vienna | Groundbreaking neuropsychiatric study examining dynamic mental body schema, phantom limbs, and social touch. | `neuropsychology` |
| 551 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1927 | Bluma Zeigarnik: Recall of Interrupted Unfinished Tasks | Bluma Zeigarnik & Kurt Lewin, Univ of Berlin | Proves unfinished tasks maintain quasi-need tension in memory and are recalled twice as well. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 552 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1927 | Freud: The Future of an Illusion | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Characterizes religious belief as universal obsessional neurosis born of infantile longing for protective father. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 553 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1927 | Ivan Pavlov: Conditioned Reflexes English Translation | Ivan Pavlov & G.V. Anrep, Oxford | English translation of Pavlovian conditioning experiments sparks massive behaviorist revolution worldwide. | `behaviorism` |
| 554 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1927 | Julius Wagner-Jauregg: Nobel Prize for Malaria Fever Cure | Julius Wagner-Jauregg, Vienna | First psychiatrist awarded Nobel Prize after curing neurosyphilitic paresis with malaria-induced fever. | `clinical_psych` |
| 555 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1927 | Wilhelm Reich: The Function of the Orgasm Published | Wilhelm Reich, Vienna | Argues neurosis stems from unreleased somatic sexual energy, pioneering character analysis and body therapy. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 556 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1928 | Anna Freud: Introduction to the Technique of Child Analysis | Anna Freud, Vienna | Formulates pedagogical and therapeutic balance necessary when analyzing young pediatric neuroses. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 557 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1928 | Edward Glover: The Technique of Psycho-Analysis | Edward Glover, British Psychoanalytical | Surveys British analysts on clinical technique, exposing vast variations in interpretation and duration. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 558 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1928 | Margaret Mead: Coming of Age in Samoa | Margaret Mead, American Museum | Anthropological fieldwork argues adolescent turmoil is cultural artifact, not universal biological inevitability. | `cultural_psych` |
| 559 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1928 | Philip Bard: Thalamic Theory of Emotion (Cannon-Bard) | Walter Cannon & Philip Bard, Harvard | Shows thalamic discharges simultaneously trigger cortical emotional feeling and autonomic arousal. | `neuropsychology` |
| 560 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1929 | Alfred Adler: The Science of Living and Individual | Alfred Adler, New York Lectures | Summarizes lifestyle, birth order, early childhood memories, and social interest for American clinicians. | `clinical_psych` |
| 561 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1929 | Karl Lashley: Brain Mechanisms and Intelligence | Karl Lashley, Chicago | Ablates rat cortex searching for memory engram, formulating mass action and equipotentiality laws. | `neuropsychology` |
| 562 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1929 | Walter Cannon: Bodily Changes in Pain, Hunger, Fear and Rage | Walter Cannon, Harvard Medical | Introduces fight-or-flight sympathetic response and homeostasis, challenging James-Lange emotion theory. | `neuropsychology` |
| 563 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1930 | Franz Alexander: Psychoanalysis of the Total Personality | Franz Alexander, Berlin & Chicago | Pioneers psychosomatic medicine: links emotional repression directly to gastrointestinal ulcers and asthma. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 564 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1930 | Freud: Civilization and Its Discontents Masterpiece | Sigmund Freud, Vienna | Tragic synthesis: civilization demands instinctual repression of aggression, generating inescapable neurotic guilt. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 565 | 5. Psychoanalytic Revolution | 1930 | Melanie Klein: Symbol Formation in Early Ego Development | Melanie Klein, British Psychoanalytical | Analyzes Dick, a severely withdrawn autistic child, demonstrating symbol formation originates in primitive anxiety. | `psychoanalysis` |

## 6. Behaviorist Era (#566 – #710)

| # | Era | Year / Period | Topic Title | Key Figure(s) / Institution | 1-Sentence Core Summary | Subfield |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 566 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1930 | B.F. Skinner: The Operant Conditioning Chamber (Skinner Box) | B.F. Skinner, Harvard Univ | Automated chamber delivers food pellets on lever press, quantifying rate of operant response. | `behaviorism` |
| 567 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1930 | Tolman & Honzik: Latent Learning in Unrewarded Maze Rats | Edward C. Tolman & C.H. Honzik, UC Berkeley | Unrewarded rats silently learn maze layouts and immediately match rewarded rats once food appears. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 568 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1931 | Winthrop Kellogg: Raising Chimpanzee Gua with Infant Son | Winthrop & Luella Kellogg | Comparative rearing study aborted when human infant begins imitating chimpanzee vocalizations. | `developmental_psych` |
| 569 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1932 | Clark Hull: The Goal-Gradient Hypothesis in Maze Learning | Clark L. Hull, Yale University | Proves rats run progressively faster and make fewer errors the closer they get to the final goal reward. | `behaviorism` |
| 570 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1932 | Edward Tolman: Purposive Behavior in Animals and Men | Edward Tolman, UC Berkeley | Cognitive behaviorism: rats in mazes acquire latent cognitive maps without immediate reinforcement. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 571 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1932 | Frederic Bartlett: Remembering and the War of the Ghosts | Frederic Bartlett, Cambridge | Pioneers schema theory: cultural expectations distort story recall toward familiar cultural norms. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 572 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1932 | Jacob L. Moreno: Psychodrama and Sociometric Networks | Jacob Levy Moreno, New York | Introduces group psychotherapy, role-playing Psychodrama, and Sociograms mapping social attraction. | `social_psych` |
| 573 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1932 | Jean Piaget: The Moral Judgment of the Child | Jean Piaget, Geneva | Traces moral development from heteronomous obedience to autonomous social reciprocity rules. | `developmental_psych` |
| 574 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1932 | Rensis Likert: The 5-Point Likert Attitude Scale | Rensis Likert, Columbia Univ | Landmark psychometric scale measuring psychological attitudes from strongly disagree to agree. | `social_psych` |
| 575 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1932 | Walter Cannon: The Wisdom of the Body and Homeostasis | Walter Bradford Cannon, Harvard Medical | Popularizes Homeostasis to explain how autonomic feedback preserves internal physiological stability. | `neuropsychology` |
| 576 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1933 | Elton Mayo: The Hawthorne Studies and Observer Effect | Elton Mayo & Fritz Roethlisberger | Demonstrates Einstellung effect: prior success with complex water-jar steps blinds to simple solutions. | `industrial_org` |
| 577 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1933 | Hedwig von Restorff: The Isolation Effect (Von Restorff) | Hedwig von Restorff, Berlin | Proves an item that noticeably stands out from surrounding list is disproportionately remembered. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 578 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1933 | Lazarsfeld, Jahoda & Zeisel: Marienthal Unemployment | Marie Jahoda & Paul Lazarsfeld, Vienna | Field study shows mass unemployment erodes time perception and induces apathetic social resignation. | `social_psych` |
| 579 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1933 | Manfred Sakel: Insulin Coma Therapy in Psychiatry | Manfred Sakel, Vienna Clinic | Introduces insulin-induced hypoglycemic coma treatments for schizophrenia patients across hospitals. | `clinical_psych` |
| 580 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1933 | Sándor Ferenczi: Identification with the Aggressor | Sándor Ferenczi, Wiesbaden Congress | Confusion of Tongues paper reveals traumatized children dissociate and internalize abuser guilt. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 581 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1933 | Wilhelm Reich: Character Analysis and Body Armor | Wilhelm Reich, Berlin Psychoanalytic | Argues chronic somatic muscular tensions (character armor) physically entrench neurotic defenses. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 582 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1934 | Karl Popper: Empirical Falsifiability and Psychology | Karl Popper, Vienna | The Logic of Scientific Discovery establishes falsifiability as the test of scientific theories. | `philosophy_of_mind` |
| 583 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1934 | Lev Vygotsky: Thought and Language and the ZPD | Lev Vygotsky, Moscow | Zone of Proximal Development: intellectual growth occurs via social scaffolding and inner speech. | `developmental_psych` |
| 584 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1934 | Richard LaPiere: Attitudes vs Actions Gap Experiment | Richard T. LaPiere, Stanford University | Shows 99% of US hotels serve a Chinese couple in person while 92% refuse in written questionnaires. | `social_psych` |
| 585 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1934 | Ruth Benedict: Patterns of Culture and Cultural Relativism | Ruth Benedict, Columbia Univ | Compares Zuni, Dobu, and Kwakiutl personalities, proving culture molds normal and deviant behavior. | `cultural_psych` |
| 586 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | Edwin R. Guthrie: One-Trial Contiguity Learning Theory | Edwin Ray Guthrie, Univ of Washington | Argues a stimulus pattern gains its full associative bond with a movement on its very first pairing. | `behaviorism` |
| 587 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | Egas Moniz: Invention of the Prefrontal Leucotomy | Egas Moniz & Almeida Lima, Lisbon | Surgeons inject alcohol into frontal lobes of psychiatric patients, founding controversial psychosurgery. | `clinical_psych` |
| 588 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | Henry Murray & Christiana Morgan: The Thematic Apperception | Henry Murray & Christiana Morgan | TAT projective test uses ambiguous interpersonal cards to reveal unconscious needs and motives. | `clinical_psych` |
| 589 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | J. Ridley Stroop: The Stroop Effect Interference | J. Ridley Stroop, George Peabody College | Demonstrates automatic word reading dramatically slows reaction time in naming mismatched ink colors. | `experimental_psych` |
| 590 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | Konrad Lorenz: Critical Period Imprinting in Goslings | Konrad Lorenz, Altenberg Austria | Demonstrates newly hatched goslings form an irreversible bond to the first moving object encountered. | `evolutionary_psych` |
| 591 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | Kurt Koffka: Principles of Gestalt Psychology Published | Kurt Koffka, Smith College | Definitive English synthesis of Gestalt laws of perceptual organization: closure, proximity, continuity. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 592 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | Kurt Lewin: A Dynamic Theory of Personality | Kurt Lewin, Univ of Iowa | Field theory models human behavior as a function of person interacting with environment: B = f(P, E). | `social_psych` |
| 593 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1935 | Melanie Klein: Paranoid-Schizoid and Depressive Positions | Melanie Klein, British Psychoanalytical | Outlines infant progression from splitting good/bad part-objects to whole-object depressive repair. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 594 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1936 | Anna Freud: The Ego and the Mechanisms of Defence | Anna Freud, Vienna | Master catalog of ego defenses: repression, reaction formation, regression, projection, and sublimation. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 595 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1936 | Hans Selye: A Syndrome Produced by Diverse Nocuous Agents | Hans Selye, Nature Journal | Discovers General Adaptation Syndrome (GAS): alarm, resistance, and exhaustion stages of stress. | `neuropsychology` |
| 596 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1936 | Jacques Lacan: The Mirror Stage of Infant Ego Formation | Jacques Lacan, Marienbad IPA Congress | Proposes infants aged 6 to 18 months form an Imaginary Ego by identifying with their mirror image. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 597 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1936 | Jean Piaget: Sensorimotor Stage and Object Permanence | Jean Piaget, Univ of Geneva | The Origins of Intelligence in Children maps assimilation, accommodation, and object permanence. | `developmental_psych` |
| 598 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1936 | Muzafer Sherif: The Autokinetic Effect and Group Norms | Muzafer Sherif, Columbia Univ | Stationary pinpoint of light in dark room appears to move; group discussion converges on shared norm. | `social_psych` |
| 599 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1936 | Walter Freeman: First Transorbital Lobotomy in America | Walter Freeman & James Watts, DC | Freeman popularizes icepick lobotomy, performing thousands of quick non-surgical outpatient procedures. | `clinical_psych` |
| 600 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1937 | Charles Bradley: Benzedrine Calms Hyperactive Children | Charles Bradley, Emma Pendleton Home RI | Discovers stimulant medication paradoxically improves attention and calms hyperactive child behavior. | `clinical_psych` |
| 601 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1937 | Gordon Allport: Personality: A Psychological Interpretation | Gordon Allport, Harvard Univ | Defines trait psychology: cardinal, central, and secondary traits shaping unique individual character. | `social_psych` |
| 602 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1937 | James Papez: The Limbic Papez Circuit of Emotion | James W. Papez, Cornell University | Identifies the anatomical brain loop connecting hippocampus, thalamus, and cingulate cortex in emotion. | `neuropsychology` |
| 603 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1937 | Karen Horney: The Neurotic Personality of Our Time | Karen Horney, New York | Frames neurosis around basic anxiety from childhood isolation, leading to moving toward, against, or away. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 604 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1937 | Penfield & Boldrey: The Cortical Sensory-Motor Homunculus | Wilder Penfield & Edwin Boldrey, Montreal | Charts the disproportionate topographic body map across human precentral and postcentral gyri. | `neuropsychology` |
| 605 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1938 | B.F. Skinner: The Behavior of Organisms Landmark Text | B.F. Skinner, Minnesota | Establishes operant behavior analysis, contrasting respondent Pavlovian reflexes with emitted operants. | `behaviorism` |
| 606 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1938 | Edmund Jacobson: Progressive Muscle Relaxation Method | Edmund Jacobson, Univ of Chicago | Demonstrates systematic tensing and releasing of muscle groups lowers autonomic nervous anxiety. | `clinical_psych` |
| 607 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1938 | Henry Murray: Explorations in Personality (Needs System) | Henry Murray, Harvard Psych Clinic | Classifies 20 psychogenic needs (achievement, affiliation, power) and environmental press forces. | `social_psych` |
| 608 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1938 | John C. Raven: Raven's Progressive Matrices IQ Test | John C. Raven, Dumfries Scotland | Develops nonverbal abstract pattern matrices to measure fluid reasoning across diverse languages. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 609 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1938 | Lauretta Bender: The Bender Visual-Motor Gestalt Test | Lauretta Bender, Bellevue Hospital NY | Introduces figure-copying test to assess perceptual-motor maturation and neurological brain damage. | `clinical_psych` |
| 610 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1938 | Louis Thurstone: Primary Mental Abilities Factor Analysis | Louis Leon Thurstone, Chicago | Challenges Spearmans single g-factor, identifying 7 distinct primary mental abilities via factor math. | `experimental_psych` |
| 611 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1938 | Ugo Cerletti & Lucio Bini: Electroconvulsive Shock Therapy | Ugo Cerletti & Lucio Bini, Rome | First therapeutic application of controlled electric shocks to human temples to induce healing convulsions. | `clinical_psych` |
| 612 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1939 | David Wechsler: The Wechsler-Bellevue Intelligence Scale | David Wechsler, Bellevue Hospital NY | Replaces mental age ratio with deviation IQ, integrating verbal and performance non-verbal subtests. | `clinical_psych` |
| 613 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1939 | Heinrich Klüver & Paul Bucy: Amygdala Lesion Syndrome | Heinrich Klüver & Paul Bucy, Univ of Chicago | Bilateral temporal lobectomy in monkeys abolishes fear and causes psychic blindness and hyperorality. | `neuropsychology` |
| 614 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1939 | Heinz Hartmann: Conflict-Free Ego Sphere and Adaptation | Heinz Hartmann, Vienna & New York | Founds Ego Psychology, arguing perception, memory, and motility mature outside neurotic conflict. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 615 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1939 | John Dollard: Frustration-Aggression Hypothesis | Dollard, Doob, Miller, Mowrer & Sears | Classic Yale formulation: aggression is always a consequence of frustration, and frustration leads to aggression. | `social_psych` |
| 616 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1939 | Kurt Lewin: Autocratic, Democratic, and Laissez-Faire | Kurt Lewin, Ronald Lippitt & White | Experimental study of boy clubs demonstrates democratic leadership yields highest cooperative morale. | `social_psych` |
| 617 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1939 | Wendell Johnson & Mary Tudor: The Monster Study Controversy | Wendell Johnson & Mary Tudor, Iowa | Unethical conditioning experiment induces persistent speech anxiety and stuttering in normal orphan children. | `behaviorism` |
| 618 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1940 | Clark Hull: Mathematico-Deductive Theory of Rote Learning | Clark Hull et al., Yale Univ | Rigorous quantitative drive-reduction behaviorism aiming to express all learning via mathematical postulates. | `behaviorism` |
| 619 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1940 | Edwin Boring: History of Experimental Psychology at Harvard | Edwin G. Boring, Harvard | Standard history cementing Wundt, Titchener, and laboratory tradition as dominant lineage of psychology. | `experimental_psych` |
| 620 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1940 | William H. Sheldon: Endomorph, Mesomorph, Ectomorph | William Herbert Sheldon, Harvard Univ | Classifies human body builds into three somatotypes and correlates them with temperament traits. | `social_psych` |
| 621 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1941 | Erich Fromm: Escape from Freedom Published | Erich Fromm, New York | Socio-psychoanalytic analysis of why modern alienated individuals surrender autonomy to totalitarian leaders. | `social_psych` |
| 622 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1941 | Miller & Dollard: Social Learning and Imitation Theory | Neal E. Miller & John Dollard, Yale Univ | Proves social imitation is learned through drive reduction, environmental cues, and reinforcement. | `behaviorism` |
| 623 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1942 | Abraham Luchins: Water Jar Einstellung Mental Set | Abraham S. Luchins, Yeshiva University | Shows prior success with a complex 3-jar formula blinds problem-solvers to an obvious direct solution. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 624 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1942 | Carl Rogers: Counseling and Psychotherapy Published | Carl Rogers, Ohio State Univ | Founds client-centered humanistic therapy: therapist provides empathic reflection rather than directive advice. | `clinical_psych` |
| 625 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1942 | Walter Cannon: Psychogenic Voodoo Death Physiology | Walter Bradford Cannon, Harvard | Explains sudden psychogenic death from extreme terror as fatal sympathetic-adrenal vascular shock. | `neuropsychology` |
| 626 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1943 | Clark Hull: Principles of Behavior Drive-Reduction Model | Clark Hull, Yale Institute | Habit strength (sHr) multiplies with drive (D) and incentive (K) to determine observable reaction potential. | `behaviorism` |
| 627 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1943 | Kenneth Craik: The Nature of Explanation and Mental Models | Kenneth Craik, Cambridge Univ | Foreshadows cognitive science: mind constructs miniature internal working models of reality to anticipate events. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 628 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1943 | Leo Kanner: Autistic Disturbances of Affective Contact | Leo Kanner, Johns Hopkins | First clinical description of 11 children with early infantile autism, noting obsessive desire for sameness. | `clinical_psych` |
| 629 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1943 | Maslow: Theory of Human Motivation (Hierarchy of Needs) | Abraham Maslow, Brooklyn College | Introduces pyramid of human needs ascending from physiological survival to safety, love, esteem, and self-actualization. | `positive_psych` |
| 630 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1943 | McCulloch & Pitts: Logical Calculus of Neural Networks | Warren McCulloch & Walter Pitts, Chicago | First mathematical model of binary threshold neurons proving neural nets can compute logic functions. | `ai_psych` |
| 631 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1943 | Raymond Cattell: Fluid vs Crystallized Intelligence | Raymond Bernard Cattell, Harvard Univ | Distinguishes innate biological Fluid reasoning (Gf) from culturally learned Crystallized skill (Gc). | `cognitive_psych` |
| 632 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1943 | Starke Hathaway & J.C. McKinley: The MMPI Published | Starke Hathaway & J.C. McKinley, MN | Minnesota Multiphasic Personality Inventory uses empirical criterion keying to diagnose psychiatric syndromes. | `clinical_psych` |
| 633 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1944 | Ancel Keys: Minnesota Starvation Psychopathology Study | Ancel Keys & Josef Brožek, Univ of Minnesota | 36 conscientious objectors undergo semi-starvation, developing severe depression and food obsession. | `clinical_psych` |
| 634 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1944 | Fritz Heider & Marianne Simmel: Attributing Intent to Shapes | Fritz Heider & Marianne Simmel, Smith | Participants perceive geometric triangles moving on screen as living characters with jealousy, anger, and intent. | `social_psych` |
| 635 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1944 | Hans Asperger: Autistic Psychopathy in Childhood | Hans Asperger, Univ of Vienna | Describes high-functioning children with intact language but severe non-verbal communication deficits. | `clinical_psych` |
| 636 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1944 | Von Neumann & Morgenstern: Game Theory and Utility | John von Neumann & Oskar Morgenstern, Princeton | Axiomatizes expected utility maximization and strategic decision-making in competitive interactions. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 637 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1945 | Karl Duncker: The Candle Problem and Functional Fixedness | Karl Duncker, Berlin & Swarthmore | Demonstrates cognitive bias where seeing a box only as a tack container blocks using it as a ledge. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 638 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1945 | Kurt Lewin: Research Center for Group Dynamics at MIT | Kurt Lewin, Cambridge MA | Pioneers action research, T-groups, and sensitivity training to resolve racial prejudice and group conflict. | `social_psych` |
| 639 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1945 | Rene Spitz: Hospitalism and Anaclitic Depression in Infants | Rene Spitz, Psychoanalytic Study of Child | Shows institutionalized infants deprived of maternal emotional bonding suffer severe developmental wasting and death. | `developmental_psych` |
| 640 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1946 | Fritz Heider: P-O-X Cognitive Balance Theory Published | Fritz Heider, Smith College | Proposes interpersonal attitude triads naturally shift toward balanced psychological harmony. | `social_psych` |
| 641 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1946 | Melanie Klein: Projective Identification Mechanism | Melanie Klein, British Psychoanalytical | Introduces Projective Identification where unwanted internal feelings are induced in another person. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 642 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1946 | S.S. Stevens: Nominal, Ordinal, Interval, Ratio Scales | Stanley Smith Stevens, Harvard | Defines the four fundamental scales of measurement, setting statistical rules for psychometrics. | `experimental_psych` |
| 643 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1946 | Solomon Asch: Impressions of Personality (Warm vs Cold) | Solomon Asch, Brooklyn College | Demonstrates changing a single central trait word (warm vs cold) radically alters entire impression of a person. | `social_psych` |
| 644 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1946 | Viktor Frankl: Mans Search for Meaning (Logotherapy) | Viktor Frankl, Vienna | Survives Auschwitz concentration camp, founding Logotherapy: finding existential meaning in unavoidable suffering. | `clinical_psych` |
| 645 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1947 | Adorno, Frenkel-Brunswik, Levinson & Sanford: Authoritarian | Theodor Adorno et al., UC Berkeley | Studies authoritarian personality traits: rigid deference to authority, anti-intraception, and hostility to outgroups. | `social_psych` |
| 646 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1947 | Jerome Bruner & Cecile Goodman: Value and Need in Perception | Jerome Bruner & Cecile Goodman, Harvard | New Look psychology: poor children overestimate physical size of coins significantly more than wealthy children. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 647 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1947 | Kenneth & Mamie Clark: The Doll Test on Racial Self-Esteem | Kenneth & Mamie Clark, New York | African American children show internalized racial stigma by preferring white dolls, cited in Brown v Board. | `social_psych` |
| 648 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1947 | O. Hobart Mowrer: Two-Factor Theory of Phobic Avoidance | Orval Hobart Mowrer, Harvard Univ | Explains phobias via Pavlovian fear conditioning maintained by operant relief from avoidance. | `behaviorism` |
| 649 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1947 | Paul Fitts: Cockpit Ergonomics and Aviation Psychology | Paul M. Fitts, Wright Field Ohio | Shows aircraft crashes stem from cockpit control layout traps rather than pilot carelessness. | `industrial_org` |
| 650 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1948 | Alfred Kinsey: Sexual Behavior in the Human Male | Alfred Kinsey et al., Indiana Univ | Landmark empirical survey of 5,300 men reveals widespread prevalence of masturbation, premarital sex, and homosexuality. | `clinical_psych` |
| 651 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1948 | B.F. Skinner: Superstition in the Pigeon Experiment | B.F. Skinner, Indiana University | Pigeons fed on non-contingent timer repeat head-tossing or pirouetting, believing rituals cause food delivery. | `behaviorism` |
| 652 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1948 | B.F. Skinner: Walden Two Utopian Novel Published | B.F. Skinner, Harvard Univ | Fictional community engineered entirely around positive reinforcement, behavioral engineering, and zero punishment. | `behaviorism` |
| 653 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1948 | Bertram Forer: The Barnum-Forer Personal Validation Fallacy | Bertram R. Forer, VA Clinic Los Angeles | Students rate vague horoscope statements as highly accurate personal profiles of their own psyche. | `social_psych` |
| 654 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1948 | Claude Shannon: Information Theory and Binary Bits | Claude E. Shannon, Bell Labs | Quantifies information entropy and channel capacity in bits, inspiring cognitive processing models. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 655 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1948 | Edward Tolman: Cognitive Maps in Rats and Men Published | Edward Tolman, Psychological Review | Summarizes spatial maze navigation evidence proving internal cognitive maps guide behavior, refuting pure S-R. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 656 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1948 | Norbert Wiener: Cybernetics and Negative Feedback Loops | Norbert Wiener, MIT | Unifies biological nervous systems and self-regulating machines under circular feedback control. | `ai_psych` |
| 657 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Boulder Conference: Scientist-Practitioner Clinical | APA Committee, Boulder CO | Formalizes PhD clinical psychology curriculum requiring dual mastery of empirical research and clinical practice. | `clinical_psych` |
| 658 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Bruner & Postman: Perceptual Defense and Trick Cards | Jerome Bruner & Leo Postman, Harvard | Subjects briefly shown anomalous playing cards (red six of spades) assimilate them to expectations. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 659 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Donald Hebb: The Organization of Behavior (Hebbian Learning) | Donald O. Hebb, McGill University | Foundational neuropsychology rule: Neurons that fire together wire together, explaining synaptic plasticity. | `neuropsychology` |
| 660 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Egas Moniz Awarded Nobel Prize for Frontal Lobotomy | Nobel Committee, Stockholm | Awards Nobel Prize for psychosurgery, triggering intense lasting historical controversy as procedures escalate. | `clinical_psych` |
| 661 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Gilbert Ryle: The Concept of Mind and Ghost in the Machine | Gilbert Ryle, Oxford Univ | Refutes Cartesian dualism as a category mistake, deriding the concept of a separate ghost in the bodily machine. | `philosophy_of_mind` |
| 662 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Harry Harlow: Learning Sets (Learning How to Learn) | Harry F. Harlow, Univ of Wisconsin | Monkeys solving hundreds of discrimination tasks transition from trial-and-error to instant insight. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 663 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | John Cade: Discovery of Lithium for Manic-Depression | John Cade, Melbourne Australia | Discovers lithium carbonate calms acute mania, launching modern pharmacological mood stabilization. | `clinical_psych` |
| 664 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Moruzzi & Magoun: Brainstem Reticular Activating System | Giuseppe Moruzzi & Horace Magoun, Northwestern | Discovers brainstem reticular formation governs cortical EEG wakefulness, alertness, and coma. | `neuropsychology` |
| 665 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1949 | Raymond Cattell: 16 Personality Factors (16PF) Taxonomy | Raymond B. Cattell, Univ of Illinois | Applies factor analysis to lexical trait descriptors, isolating 16 primary source traits of personality. | `social_psych` |
| 666 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1950 | Alan Turing: Computing Machinery and the Imitation Game | Alan Turing, Mind Journal | Proposes the Turing Test for machine intelligence, launching computational philosophy of mind and AI psychology. | `ai_psych` |
| 667 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1950 | Dollard & Miller: Personality and Psychotherapy Synthesis | John Dollard & Neal E. Miller, Yale Univ | Translates Freudian repression and approach-avoidance conflict gradients into behavioral learning. | `behaviorism` |
| 668 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1950 | Erik Erikson: Childhood and Society (8 Psychosocial Stages) | Erik Erikson, San Francisco Psychoanalytic | Expands Freuds psychosexual stages across entire lifespan from infant trust vs mistrust to elder ego integrity. | `developmental_psych` |
| 669 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1950 | Theodor Adorno: The Authoritarian Personality Published | Theodor Adorno et al., New York | Comprehensive psychodynamic and psychometric profile of prejudice, rigid conformity, and ethnocentrism. | `social_psych` |
| 670 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1951 | Carl Rogers: Client-Centered Therapy Principles Formalized | Carl Rogers, Univ of Chicago | Defines necessary therapeutic conditions: unconditional positive regard, congruence, and empathic understanding. | `clinical_psych` |
| 671 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1951 | Fritz Perls: Gestalt Therapy and Growth of Personality | Fritz Perls, Ralph Hefferline & Goodman | Pioneers experiential Gestalt therapy focusing on here-and-now somatic awareness, empty chair, and contact boundary. | `clinical_psych` |
| 672 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1951 | John Bowlby: Maternal Care and Mental Health WHO Report | John Bowlby, Tavistock Clinic London | Groundbreaking WHO monograph arguing warm, continuous infant-mother bond is indispensable for mental health. | `developmental_psych` |
| 673 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1951 | Karl Lashley: The Problem of Serial Order in Behavior | Karl S. Lashley, Hixon Symposium Caltech | Refutes associative reflex chaining, proving rapid speech and music require hierarchical motor plans. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 674 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1951 | Lee J. Cronbach: Coefficient Alpha Test Reliability | Lee J. Cronbach, Univ of Illinois | Formulates Cronbach's Alpha as the universal psychometric measure of internal scale consistency. | `experimental_psych` |
| 675 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1951 | Nikolaas Tinbergen: The Study of Instinct and Ethology | Nikolaas Tinbergen, Oxford University | Demonstrates innate releasing mechanisms and supernormal stimuli in sticklebacks and herring gulls. | `evolutionary_psych` |
| 676 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1951 | Solomon Asch: Line Judgment Social Conformity Experiment | Solomon Asch, Swarthmore College | 75% of participants conform to unanimously incorrect confederates on simple visual line length judgment. | `social_psych` |
| 677 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1952 | APA Publishes DSM-I: First Diagnostic and Statistical Manual | American Psychiatric Association, DC | Classifies 106 mental disorders grounded in Adolf Meyers psychobiological reaction model. | `clinical_psych` |
| 678 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1952 | Chlorpromazine (Thorazine): Dawn of Psychopharmacology | Delay, Deniker & Laborit, Paris | First antipsychotic drug dramatically calms manic psychosis, initiating psychiatric deinstitutionalization. | `clinical_psych` |
| 679 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1952 | Frantz Fanon: Black Skin, White Masks Racism Psychoanalysis | Frantz Fanon, Lyon & Martinique | Profound clinical critique analyzing psychological alienation, self-hatred, and neurosis inflicted by colonial racism. | `cultural_psych` |
| 680 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1952 | Hans Eysenck: The Effects of Psychotherapy Evaluation Shock | Hans Eysenck, London | Meta-analytic critique alleging two-thirds of neurotic patients recover spontaneously without psychotherapy. | `clinical_psych` |
| 681 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1952 | Hodgkin & Huxley: Ionic Model of the Action Potential | Alan Hodgkin & Andrew Huxley, Cambridge | Squid giant axon voltage-clamp equations prove sodium and potassium channels drive nerve impulses. | `neuropsychology` |
| 682 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1953 | B.F. Skinner: Science and Human Behavior Textbook | B.F. Skinner, Harvard University | Comprehensive treatise applying operant behavioral principles to government, law, education, and religion. | `behaviorism` |
| 683 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1953 | CIA Project MKUltra Mind Control Program Launched | Sidney Gottlieb & CIA, Washington DC | Covert unethical human experiments administering LSD, sensory deprivation, and hypnosis to unwitting subjects. | `clinical_psych` |
| 684 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1953 | Eugene Aserinsky & Nathaniel Kleitman: REM Sleep Discovery | Eugene Aserinsky & Nathaniel Kleitman | Discovers rapid eye movement (REM) sleep accompanied by electroencephalographic dreaming brainwaves. | `experimental_psych` |
| 685 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1953 | Kinsey: Statistical Survey of Female Sexual Behavior | Alfred Kinsey, Indiana University | Surveys female sexual response, debunking vaginal orgasm myth and demonstrating sexual equality in desire. | `clinical_psych` |
| 686 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1953 | Patient H.M. (Henry Molaison): Bilateral Medial Temporal | William Scoville & Brenda Milner | Surgical removal of hippocampi causes dense anterograde amnesia, proving hippocampus consolidates new memories. | `neuropsychology` |
| 687 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1954 | Abraham Maslow: Motivation and Personality Published | Abraham Maslow, Brandeis University | Outlines psychological profile of self-actualizing individuals: peak experiences, autonomy, and deep empathy. | `positive_psych` |
| 688 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1954 | Bexton, Heron & Scott: McGill Sensory Deprivation Study | Woodburn Heron & Donald Hebb, McGill Univ | Volunteers isolated in soundproof cubicles with translucent goggles experience vivid hallucinations. | `experimental_psych` |
| 689 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1954 | Gordon Allport: The Nature of Prejudice Masterpiece | Gordon Allport, Harvard University | Definitive social analysis of stereotyping, in-group favoritism, and the Contact Hypothesis to reduce bias. | `social_psych` |
| 690 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1954 | James Olds & Peter Milner: Reward Centers in the Brain | James Olds & Peter Milner, McGill Univ | Rats press levers thousands of times per hour to deliver direct electrical stimulation to septal pleasure centers. | `neuropsychology` |
| 691 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1954 | Julian Rotter: Social Learning Theory and Locus of Control | Julian Rotter, Univ of Connecticut | Introduces internal vs external locus of control: individual beliefs regarding personal agency vs external fate. | `social_psych` |
| 692 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1954 | Muzafer Sherif: Robbers Cave Intergroup Conflict Experiment | Muzafer Sherif, Oklahoma State Park | Eagles and Rattlers boys camp groups descend into fierce hostility, resolved only through superordinate goals. | `social_psych` |
| 693 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1954 | Paul Meehl: Clinical vs Statistical Prediction | Paul E. Meehl, Univ of Minnesota | Demonstrates actuarial statistical algorithms consistently outperform clinical intuition in forecasting outcomes. | `clinical_psych` |
| 694 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Albert Ellis: Rational Emotive Behavior Therapy (REBT) | Albert Ellis, New York City | Pioneers cognitive therapy via ABC model: Activating events provoke irrational Beliefs causing emotional Consequences. | `clinical_psych` |
| 695 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | B.F. Skinner: Reinforcement Schedules and Ratio Breakpoints | B.F. Skinner & Charles Ferster | Comprehensive laboratory atlas tracking animal response curves under fixed/variable interval and ratio schedules. | `behaviorism` |
| 696 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Brenda Milner: Intellectual Function of the Temporal Lobes | Brenda Milner, McGill University | Detailed psychometric assessment of unilateral temporal lobectomy patients during Montreal Neurological series. | `neuropsychology` |
| 697 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | D.T. Suzuki & Erich Fromm: Zen Buddhism and Psychoanalysis | D.T. Suzuki & Erich Fromm, Cuernavaca | Landmark cross-cultural conference linking Zen satori awakening with psychoanalytic unconscious insight. | `cultural_psych` |
| 698 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Donald Broadbent: Dichotic Listening Selective Attention | Donald Broadbent, Cambridge MRC | Uses headphones playing separate messages to prove selective attention bottleneck filters out unattended speech. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 699 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Erich Fromm: The Sane Society and Alienation Critique | Erich Fromm, New York | Critiques capitalist consumption culture, arguing conformist modern society induces mass neurosis and emptiness. | `social_psych` |
| 700 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | George Kelly: The Psychology of Personal Constructs | George Kelly, Ohio State University | Proposes humans act as intuitive scientists, interpreting reality through bipolar personal cognitive construct grids. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 701 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Hans Eysenck: The Dimensions of Personality (PEN Model) | Hans Eysenck, Maudsley Hospital London | Biological trait model anchoring Extraversion and Neuroticism in reticular ascending system arousal levels. | `neuropsychology` |
| 702 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Herbert Simon: Bounded Rationality and Satisficing Decisions | Herbert Simon, Carnegie Mellon | Challenges homo economicus, proving human decision-makers satisfice within cognitive memory constraints. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 703 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | J.P. Guilford: The Structure of Intellect Model | Joy Paul Guilford, USC | Proposes multidimensional cube model classifying 120 distinct mental abilities, distinguishing divergent thinking. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 704 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | John Bowlby: Attachment and Loss Formulation at Tavistock | John Bowlby, Tavistock London | Integrates ethology, evolutionary biology, and psychoanalysis into rigorous modern infant attachment theory. | `developmental_psych` |
| 705 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Joseph Wolpe: Reciprocal Inhibition in Psychotherapy | Joseph Wolpe, Johannesburg South Africa | Proves anxiety can be inhibited by incompatible responses (relaxation), inventing systematic desensitization. | `clinical_psych` |
| 706 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Korean Hwa-Byung: Cultural Somatization of Repressed Rage | Korean Traditional Neuropsychiatry | Identifies culture-bound syndrome characterized by epigastric constriction and hot flashes from unfair suffering. | `cultural_psych` |
| 707 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Lee Cronbach & Paul Meehl: Construct Validity in Tests | Lee Cronbach & Paul Meehl, Psych Bulletin | Formalizes theoretical construct validity: validating unobservable psychological traits through nomological networks. | `experimental_psych` |
| 708 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Shoma Morita: Morita Therapy for Shinkeishitsu Neurosis | Shoma Morita, Jikei Univ Tokyo | Japanese ecological therapy: four-stage bed rest and work therapy guiding acceptance of anxiety (Aruga-mama). | `cultural_psych` |
| 709 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Wilder Penfield: The Interpretive Cortex and Flashback | Wilder Penfield, Montreal Neurological | Direct electrical stimulation of temporal cortex evokes vivid multi-sensory childhood memory flashbacks. | `neuropsychology` |
| 710 | 6. Behaviorist Era | 1955 | Yoshimoto Ishin: Naikan Therapy Self-Reflection Method | Yoshimoto Ishin, Nara Japan | Structured introspection examining three questions: what was received, what was given, and what troubles caused. | `cultural_psych` |

## 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave (#711 – #825)

| # | Era | Year / Period | Topic Title | Key Figure(s) / Institution | 1-Sentence Core Summary | Subfield |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 711 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Allen Newell & Herbert Simon: The Logic Theorist Computer | Allen Newell & Herbert Simon, RAND | First running AI program proves mathematical theorems, establishing physical symbol systems in cognitive science. | `ai_psych` |
| 712 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Bruner, Goodnow & Austin: A Study of Thinking Concept Rules | Jerome Bruner, Jacqueline Goodnow & Austin | Pioneers experimental study of hypothesis testing and cognitive strategies used to acquire abstract concepts. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 713 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Dartmouth Summer Conference: Founding of Artificial | McCarthy, Minsky, Rochester & Shannon | Seminal conference coining Artificial Intelligence and launching computational simulations of cognitive mind. | `ai_psych` |
| 714 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Donald Winnicott: Primary Maternal Preoccupation Concept | Donald Winnicott, London Institute | Identifies adaptive maternal attunement state essential for infant ego integration and psychological security. | `psychoanalysis` |
| 715 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Erich Fromm: The Art of Loving Published | Erich Fromm, New York | Classic psychological inquiry framing mature love not as passive falling but as an active art requiring discipline. | `positive_psych` |
| 716 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | George Miller: The Magical Number Seven, Plus or Minus Two | George A. Miller, Harvard Univ | Seminal cognitive paper proving human short-term working memory capacity is strictly limited to 7±2 chunks. | `cognitive_psych` |
| 717 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Gregory Bateson: Double Bind Hypothesis of Schizophrenia | Gregory Bateson, Don Jackson & Haley | Proposes paradoxical contradictory family communication patterns trap vulnerable children into psychotic breaks. | `clinical_psych` |
| 718 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Jack Brehm: Post-Decision Dissonance Free-Choice Paradigm | Jack W. Brehm, Yale University | Choosing between two attractive appliances causes people to upgrade the chosen item and downgrade the rejected one. | `social_psych` |
| 719 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1956 | Leon Festinger: When Prophecy Fails (Cognitive Dissonance) | Leon Festinger, Henry Riecken & Schachter | Dynamic testing evaluates cognitive D-factor, learning potential, and modifiability in children. | `social_psych` |
| 720 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1957 | Alexander Thomas & Stella Chess: New York Longitudinal Study | Alexander Thomas & Stella Chess, NYU | Pioneering developmental tracking of infant temperament styles: easy, difficult, and slow-to-warm-up. | `developmental_psych` |
| 721 | 7. Gestalt & Humanistic Wave | 1957 | B.F. Skinner: Verbal Behavior Published | B.F. Skinner, Harvard University | Analyzes human language strictly as operant behavior composed of mands, tacts, echoics, and intraverbals. | `behaviorism` |
